- Deliberately left out: M3U health-probe (HEAD) refusals (not the Trex path); the two M3U fetches that still send no User-Agent; no retry/backoff change for 403s (a separate fix, not a recorder's job). Image-fetch refusals: NOT included (they do not ride the API-client path; the brief made them optional).
- Version: 1.0.8-2.25.0+180. Changelog `about.changelog2272` (2271 is build #24's, built in parallel off the same base — rebased onto #24's tip 7fd694f65: both changelog keys kept, pubspec 1.0.8-2.25.0+180, FORK §23 = #24, §24 = this). UNPROVEN (phone-only): a real refusal lands in the log with its cf-ray/body intact; the Copy text is readable/useful when the owner sends it.
### 25. Block fixes — wait for release, never retry a 403, gate background work on playback, back off a refused panel (v1.0.9-2.25.0, versionCode 181)
- Why (the owner, 2026-11-10 ~07:55 EST: build the fixes right after 1.0.8 ships, don't wait for the recorder log): Trex blocks PlezTV shortly after use on a max_connections=1 line. Research `~/workspace/tmp_work/plezy-tv-research-block-after-use/RESEARCH_BLOCK_AFTER_USE.md` ranks the mechanisms from open-source peers + boards: (1) zap overlap — PlezTV kept the old stream alive until the new one opened, plus #24's 2 pre-tune GETs per zap; OwnTV measured this exact failure on a real one-connection panel ("thirteen streams in 3.6 s… locked the account for about two minutes, HTTP 458") and fixed it with a 500 ms debounce + wait-for-release; (2) retrying a refusal reads as an attack — live 403s climbed a retry ladder that refills on playback restart, and refused `player_api.php` calls count toward the flood limit; (3) ghost slot — `discard()` is a no-op and nothing tells the panel a stream ended, so the slot lingers ~30 s–5 min; (4) background EPG/catalog work runs while streaming (OwnTV's workers wait until nothing is playing); (5) the health probe on resume + the reconnect sweep re-probe a refused panel.
- Wait-for-release: NEW `lib/screens/video_player/live_stream_release_gate.dart` — `LiveStreamReleaseGate` (cap 1,500 ms, OwnTV's number): `release(stop)` awaits any in-flight release, runs stop(), awaits it with the cap, swallows errors/timeout; `waitForRelease()` awaits an in-flight release (capped). The zap path (`_switchLiveChannelNow` in `lib/screens/video_player/parts/live_tv.dart`) awaits the gate before `_openLiveStream` when the previous session requires it; `_openLiveStream` also awaits `waitForRelease()` at its top so no open can start while a release is in flight; the mini-player's `tune()` (`lib/screens/livetv/live_tv_mini_player.dart`) does the same before `player.open`. Gate is per player instance. Zap debounce: NEW `lib/screens/video_player/live_zap_debouncer.dart` — `LiveZapDebouncer` (delay 500 ms, OwnTV's number; injected clock + timer factory for tests; `onExecute` + `onDrop`): isolated press executes at once (0 ms); rapid presses accumulate the net delta and restart the timer (trailing-edge); a net-zero burst executes NOTHING (executing 0 would re-open the channel already playing) and fires `onDrop` so callers awaiting the switch are released. Wired inside `_switchLiveChannel` (returns a Future via pending completers; real logic moved to `_switchLiveChannelNow`); dispose/shutdown complete pending completers so no awaiter hangs.
- Single-connection plumbing: `IptvClient` caches `max_connections` from `getUserInfo()` in `checkHealth()` (the one place the client calls it); new `int? maxConnections` + `bool isSingleConnection` — default unknown → wait (true unless the panel told us it allows more than one; the wait is bounded by the stop completing, usually tens of ms, and the health probe runs at bind so unknown is rare and brief). Applies to Xtream AND M3U (M3U never knows → waits). NEW getter on the abstract session: `bool get requiresReleaseBeforeOpen => false` (default false — Plex/Jellyfin don't need it); `IptvLiveTvPlaybackSession` overrides it to `client.isSingleConnection`. The zap gate checks the PREVIOUS session (its panel holds the slot); the pre-tune probe gate checks the NEIGHBOUR session's own panel. Pre-tune probe gate: `shouldProbe` is now `session is IptvLiveTvPlaybackSession && !session.requiresReleaseBeforeOpen` — the neighbour is still pre-tuned (session + URL, both local) but never probed on a single-connection line; the 10 s open deadline still bounds a dead channel. Ghost slot: verified `discard()` is a no-op because the IPTV session owns no releasable resource (no panel logout/disconnect endpoint exists — no peer sends one); the fix IS the awaited player stop (release gate) in the zap/retune paths + the choke-point guard. `discard()` stays a no-op with an updated comment.
- Never retry a 403: in `resolvePlaybackFailureAction` (`lib/screens/video_player/playback_failure_action.dart`), a latched 403 returns `playbackNotAllowedDialog` FIRST (before the transient branch and before the live ladder) for both live and VOD; the VOD `retryTransient` condition keeps only 429. NEW helper `shouldRefillLiveLadderAfterRestart(Set<int>) => !fatalHttpStatuses.contains(403)`; the `playbackRestart` listener in `playback_services.dart` captures `hadRefusal` BEFORE clearing the latched sets and skips `_resetLiveLadderOnPlaybackRestart()` when a refusal was latched. **458 stays ladder-eligible on live TV** (documented): it is the transient line-busy signal ("458 is TRANSIENT" — the owner's rule), and the ladder's 1 s/2 s backoff gives the ghost slot time to expire, so a retry after the wait can succeed where a 403 retry never could. Stale comments updated in `playback_failure_action.dart`, `lib/mpv/models.dart`, `lib/screens/video_player/parts/errors.dart`.
- Playback-activity signal + background-work gating: NEW `lib/services/iptv/iptv_playback_activity.dart` — `IptvPlaybackActivity` (singleton `instance`, `@visibleForTesting debugResetInstance`, `ValueNotifier<bool> isPlayingNotifier`, `markPlaying()`/`markIdle()`, `Future<void> waitForIdle()` completing immediately when idle, all waiters completing on idle; listener-based, a parked waiter self-cleans its listener at the next `markIdle`). Set by: full player at first frame (`_markFirstFrameReady`) → playing, dispose/fatal (`_latchFatalPlaybackError`, `_shutdownVideo`) → idle; mini-player tune active → playing, stop/stopForFullscreen/fail/dispose → idle. Paused still counts as playing (the slot is held server-side until expiry). Gate the STARTS on playback — weekly sync (check before each type's fetch inside `_syncSearchIndex`; if playing, `waitForIdle()` — the sync pauses between types and resumes; the wait happens BEFORE acquiring the index-write lock so a manual refresh/seed isn't blocked; force/manual refresh skips the gate — user intent wins), catalog background refresh (`_refreshCatalogInBackground` — defer until idle, not just skip, because `_liveStreamsFuture` is cached and a skipped refresh won't re-fire; the fetch now takes `Future<void>? abortTrigger` and all 6 call sites pass it through; VOD/series use the crawl-capable loaders which don't take an abort, so a post-fetch `abort.throwIfAborted()` discards a cancelled refresh's result before any disk write/swap), xmltv feed (in `_xmltvGuideForKey`, if playing don't START a new feed download; short-EPG only; if a feed is already cached/in-flight, awaiting it is free). Per-channel short-EPG is NOT gated on playback (small, bounded — 4 concurrent, 15 s first-paint — and it is what the visible guide needs while the mini-player plays; the report gates only the weekly sync and the whole-guide download, and gives short-EPG the scroll-away cancel instead). **Cancel on background:** new `IptvClient.cancelBackgroundWork()` (aborts `_activeBackgroundAborts` — sync + catalog refresh controllers — plus `_liveTv.cancelBackgroundWork()` for EPG), `MultiServerManager.cancelActiveIptvBackgroundWork()`, wired in `main.dart` paused/detached next to `cancelActiveIptvSearches()`. **Scroll-away cancel:** abort threaded through `fetchEpgForChannel` → short-EPG/xmltv; the support tracks in-flight per-channel fetches (`_epgAbortsByChannel` map) and exposes per-channel + all cancel; the guide tab wraps each row in `_IptvEpgRowLifecycle` (StatefulWidget, stable `ValueKey(scopeKey)`, dispose cancels that channel's fetch) and its `_ensureIptvEpg` catchError now distinguishes cancellation (NOT marked failed — scrolling back refetches — vs a real failure, which still marks failed).
- Health-probe backoff: in `IptvClient.checkHealth()` (the one choke point — all paths funnel through it: `checkServerHealth`, `_reconnectIptvServer` → `_probeIptvHealth`, deferred probe): if the refusal log (`IptvRefusalLog.instance`, `entriesNotifier`, loaded via `ensureLoaded()` with try/catch → treat failure as "no recorded refusal") has a recent 403 for this connection (within **5 min**, the boards' "wait 5 min" advice, test-overridable `refusalProbeBackoff` static), return `_lastHealthStatus` (accessDenied) WITHOUT a panel call. After the window, probe normally. Unconditional (all callers, including the manual reconnect sweep) — documented; a fresh sign-in bypasses it (different path). Only 403 backs off (a 429 is rate-limiting, not a refusal). A log that cannot be loaded fails open (probe runs).
- EPG abort plumbing: `XtreamApi.getShortEpg` + `getXmltv` gained optional `abortTrigger`; `_getText` now uses `AbortableRequest` (like `_fetchBody`) so the feed aborts at the socket; `_fetchXtreamShortEpg` takes + passes the trigger (its concurrency slot releases in a `finally`, so an abort can't leak a slot); `_fetchXtreamEpg` was split into a wrapper (owns the per-channel `AbortController`, registers it in `_epgAbortsByChannel` + `_activeEpgAborts`, removes in `finally`) + `_fetchXtreamEpgWithAbort` (passes the trigger to short-EPG, rethrows cancellations instead of falling through to the feed, and `throwIfAborted()`s before awaiting the feed); the feed download is started by `_startXmltvDownload` (owns its own AbortController, registered in `_activeEpgAborts`, removed on settle — the per-channel cancel does NOT touch the shared feed); `_loadXmltvGuide` does NOT set the `_xmltvFailedAt` negative-cache marker on a cancellation; the M3U feed (`_xmltvGuide`) races its bare `http.get` against the trigger (best-effort — the socket may finish in the background; documented). The feed-deferral warning is suppressed while playing (a deferral is the gate doing its job, not a failure — logged per row otherwise). Ripple: adding the optional `abortTrigger` param to `getShortEpg`/`getXmltv` broke 15 existing fake-API overrides (`invalid_override` — Dart does not allow an override to omit an optional named parameter of the base); fixed by adding the parameter to the overrides in `iptv_categories_screen_test.dart`, `iptv_categories_screen_scroll_test.dart`, `iptv_build17_epg_coverage_test.dart`, `iptv_build22_stream_user_agent_test.dart`, `iptv_epg_cache_test.dart`, `iptv_epg_test.dart`, `iptv_screens_test.dart`, `library_header_overflow_test.dart`, `guide_tab_iptv_test.dart`, `iptv_build17_channel_menu_test.dart`, `live_tv_mini_player_test.dart`, `guide_search_sheet_test.dart`, `iptv_build17_catchup_test.dart`, `iptv_build17_mini_player_test.dart`, `live_tv_mini_player_controller_test.dart`, `live_tv_mini_player_first_frame_test.dart` (the fakes answer immediately, so they ignore it — the abort is exercised by the #28 fake that races it). Adding the `requiresReleaseBeforeOpen` getter to `LiveTvPlaybackSession` broke every `implements` class (defaults don't satisfy `implements`) — explicit `=> false` added to `_PlexLiveTvPlaybackSession`, `_JellyfinLiveTvPlaybackSession`, and 5 test fakes.
- Tests: NEW `test/screens/video_player/live_stream_release_gate_test.dart` (7), `live_zap_debouncer_test.dart` (7, incl. the onDrop test that caught the net-zero design gap — the first version executed a net-zero pending delta, re-opening the channel already playing; fixed in the debouncer), `test/services/iptv/iptv_playback_activity_test.dart` (6), `iptv_build28_block_fixes_test.dart` (12: max_connections caching 1/5/unknown; session getter; backoff recent-403 → 0 panel calls / window-expired → probe runs / no refusal → probe runs / 429 doesn't back off; sync 0 requests while playing then 6 after idle; forced refresh not gated; stale-disk browse 0 live requests while playing then 1 after idle; cancelBackgroundWork aborts an in-flight sync), `iptv_build28_epg_gate_test.dart` (3: feed doesn't start while playing, starts when idle; scroll-away cancel stops a 2 s short-EPG promptly; background cancel aborts a 5 s feed download, feed re-downloads after — no 120 s negative cache), `test/screens/video_player/live_zap_release_gate_test.dart` (2: zap waits for the old stream to release before opening the next — order stop-started < stop-finished < open; a session that does not require release opens without waiting), `test/screens/livetv/live_tv_mini_player_release_gate_test.dart` (1: retune waits for the old stream to release), `test/screens/livetv/guide_tab_epg_cancel_test.dart` (1: a guide row scrolled away cancels its in-flight EPG fetch — 30 channels, real drag, '100' aborted, '124' not, new fetches start for newly visible rows). UPDATED (deliberate policy change, stricter assertions — no weakening): `playback_failure_action_transient_test.dart` + `playback_failure_action_test.dart` (403 → `playbackNotAllowedDialog` immediately, no retry; the live ladder is still covered elsewhere: 404 → liveRetry, ladder exhaustion, in-flight retry owns the error with 404). Failing-first: all 3 new unit files fail to load on base 6fe84db64 (new APIs absent); `iptv_build28_block_fixes_test.dart` fails to load on base (missing `maxConnections`, `isSingleConnection`, `requiresReleaseBeforeOpen`, `testRefusalLog`, `refusalProbeBackoff`, `cancelBackgroundWork`, `iptv_playback_activity.dart`); `iptv_build28_epg_gate_test.dart` fails to load on base; the 3 widget files fail on base with the exact product reason (zap: base opens ch-2 with no stop at all — `openedUris` [ch-1, ch-2], no 'stop-started'; mini-player: base retunes without stopping; guide: base `aborted` is empty — the scrolled-away fetch is never aborted); the transient tests fail on base with the exact product reason (403 → `liveRetry`/`retryTransient` instead of the dialog). Widget-test notes: the zap test starts the tune in the fake zone (not runAsync) so pumps flush its microtasks — a second runAsync while the zap is pending is denied as reentrant, and fake-time pumps alone do not advance a real-zone tune; the fake player's stop is held open by a test Completer; drain the fake clock 15 s past the release gate's 1.5 s timeout and the zap's 10 s open deadline before disposing or a pending-timer assertion fails the test. The guide test's fake API must RACE the abort trigger against its Completer (like the real API's AbortableRequest at the socket) — otherwise the support stays parked on the pending future, the concurrency slot never releases, and no queued fetch starts; and the cleanup must guard `Completer.complete` with `isCompleted` (it throws on an already-completed future) and use bounded pumps, not `pumpAndSettle` (the 15 s first-paint timers keep firing). Suites: `test/screens/video_player/` 318/318, `test/screens/livetv/` 104/104, `test/services/iptv/` 340/340 (`--concurrency 2`), `test/mpv/` 193/193, `test/screens/settings/` 217 passed / 3 skipped / 1 failed — the failure is the PRE-EXISTING #20 UA widget test ("picking Custom reveals the custom text row"), proven failing on the base 6fe84db64 too (same file/line/error; #23 broke it, not #28). One real #28-caused test failure found and fixed during the suite run: `live_media_session_test.dart` "OS next/previous on live zap channels" — the no-op Previous at ch-1 executes through the debouncer and consumes the isolated-press slot, so the Next a few real milliseconds later falls inside the 500 ms rapid-press window and is deferred; the test now pumps 600 ms past the debounce window. That is the debounce working as designed (a no-op press delays the next real press by at most 500 ms), not a regression. Analyze 49 = baseline, identical issue list to base (verified by diffing the full analyze output, base worktree vs fix worktree).
- Deliberately left out: the #20 UA widget test issue (separate queue item — #23 broke it, test-only vs real reachability still to determine); no change to the refusal recorder's log format; no new panel calls, probes, or retries anywhere (the only new panel-adjacent behaviour is FEWER calls: the backoff skips a probe, the gates defer work, the cancels stop work); User-Agent behaviour (#20/#22) unchanged.
- Version: 1.0.9-2.25.0+181. Changelog `about.changelog2273`. UNPROVEN (phone-only): zapping waits for the old channel to release (no overlap) on a real one-connection line; a refused stream shows the access-denied dialog with no re-open; background work doesn't run while watching; a refused panel isn't re-probed on every resume; tap→first-frame zap time was NOT measured honestly (the mock player renders no real first frame — same caveat as #24).
### 26. One freshness rule — browse refresh routes through the sync; seed stamps real age; FTS rank order (v1.0.10-2.25.0, versionCode 182)
- Why (the owner, 2026-11-10 ~15:43 EST: "Go, build R1 + the search fixes"): the deep audit (`~/workspace/tmp_work/plezy-tv-research-deep-audit/RESEARCH_DEEP_AUDIT.md`, M1/M2/M3) found disk, memory and index disagreeing by design — a 24 h disk TTL vs a 168 h sync interval, a browse refresh that bypassed the sync guards and never wrote the index, a seed that stamped stale data as fresh, and FTS rows returned in insertion order so the 100-row cap could cut the exact match. Post-#23 report R1 is the same finding.
- M3 — one freshness rule: NEW `_refreshCatalogViaSyncIfDue(type)` (`lib/media/iptv_client.dart`) replaces `_refreshCatalogInBackground` (deleted) at all 6 Xtream call sites (live/VOD/series catalog + category loaders). After serving disk rows it fires `ensureSearchIndexSynced()` when the type is due under the sync interval (lastSync null, rows 0, or age >= interval); otherwise nothing is fetched. The sync fetches catalog + categories once, under the empty/partial/oversize guards, and writes disk + index together. Interval 0 (manual-only): no automatic refresh at all. The 24 h `catalogCacheTtl` now gates only the M3U playlist refresh (doc updated in `iptv_catalog_cache.dart`). Preserved: #28's idle gating (the sync waits while playing, cancellable on backgrounding), all #23 guards, the STARTUP RULE (app opens from the local file). M4 folded in: live gains the `<½` partial guard VOD/series already had, via the sync (the old live refresh had none). M5 folded in: `_publishSyncedCatalog(type, rows, categories)` now also sets the memoized category futures from the synced list (the index's single source) and nulls the three id -> name maps, and calls `notifyCatalogRefreshed(liveCatalogChanged: type == live)` after a publish (the old refresh's notify, preserved — the #23 sync published silently, so this is the first time a sync emits the event). The cold path (no disk file → direct network fetch, no index write) is unchanged — the bind sync covers the index; noted as a residual below.
- M1 — seed stamps real age: `_seedSearchIndexFromDisk` stamps each type with its disk file's `fetchedAt` instead of `nowMs`, so the sync's up-to-date skip reads true ages and a stale disk actually syncs instead of being stamped fresh and skipped for a week.
- M2 — FTS rank order: `IptvSearchIndex.search` gains a `{int limit = ftsQueryLimit}` parameter (new constant `ftsQueryLimit = 200`, replacing `ftsCandidateLimit = 5000`); the three `_search*FromIndex` callers pass `limit: limit * 2` (2x headroom for the caller's RegExp + hidden-category filtering). The LIKE arm gains `LIMIT ?` + `ORDER BY rowid` (it had no LIMIT at all). The FTS arm does NOT always `ORDER BY rank`: it first probes up to `ftsRankWindow` + 1 = 1001 rows in natural order — a set that fits the window (≤ 1000) is re-run ranked (cheap there, and rank order is what keeps the exact match in the cap); a set that overflows returns its first 200 probe rows unranked. Reason (measured): ranking means scoring every match — ~420 ms for 200k matches (Python sqlite3, same table shape) — and the always-ranked version pushed the #23 bench common-needle median from ~531 ms to 1535 ms (bound 1000 ms). With the probe the bench is back to 450 ms median (faster than #23's 531 ms, because the caller now scores 200 rows instead of 5000). Past ~1000 near-identical rows the rank order is noise and the caller still scores the 200 it gets.
- Tests: NEW `test/services/iptv/iptv_build29_freshness_test.dart` (8): M2 FTS rank (bare 'game of thrones' first, count ≤ 200) + LIKE bound (count ≤ 200 for 'of'); M1 seed stamps 8 days + the following sync fires (6 requests); M3a stale disk + fresh index → 0 requests; M3b stale disk + due index → 6 requests (one sync, not refresh+sync); M3c interval 0 → 0 requests; M4 partial live (10 of 100) → disk stays 100; M5 synced categories reach the memoized futures. Failing-first: 0/8 pass on base b98804227 (each with the exact product reason, verified in a scratch base worktree, removed after), 8/8 on the fix. UPDATED (deliberate behaviour change, guarded behaviour preserved): `iptv_build28_block_fixes_test.dart` stale-disk test — TTL=0 no longer makes a disk stale, so it backdates the files 8 days (gating behaviour unchanged: 0 while playing, 1 live fetch when idle); `iptv_build19_probes_test.dart` P5a — `makeDiskSeriesStale()` backdates 8 days instead of 25 h (25 h is no longer due under the 168 h interval), and P5a sets `IptvSearchIndex.debugDirectoryProvider` (scoped to that test only — the refresh now routes through the sync, which needs the index; the other probes model a cold index, so the provider stays unset for them; the sync itself returns all-'failed' when the index is null, so this dependency is #23's design, not new); `iptv_catalog_cache_test.dart` — the 3 stale-refresh tests age 8 days instead of 25 h, set the index provider at group level, seed the other types fresh (so the sync refreshes one type and emits exactly one event), the fake API now stores the raw bodies like the real XtreamApi does (the sync skips the write when the body is null — the old refresh fell back to re-encoding the rows, which masked the missing body), and the `waitFor` helper timeout went 1 s → 5 s (the sync's isolate seed is slower than the old direct fetch; the corrupted-cache test that failed under full-suite load passed alone and passes now). Suites: `test/services/iptv/` 348/348, `test/screens/livetv/` 104/104, search/aggregation (search_screen, catalog_search_screen, search_screen_build19_scope_widget, data_aggregation_bridge, debounced_media_search) 81/81, `test/screens/settings/` 218 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed — the #20 UA widget test PASSES. Analyze 49 = baseline, identical issue list to base (verified by diffing the full analyze output, base worktree vs fix worktree; one self-inflicted lint, `_seedFreshType` underscore, caught and renamed to `seedFreshType`).
- Deliberately left out / residuals: the cold path (no disk file) still fetches directly without writing the index; the client-level exact-match guarantee for decorated titles against shorter near-matches is R2's scorer work, not M2's — measured with sqlite3 on the exact query shape, BM25 ranks the real 'EN - Game Of Thrones (2011)' 251st of 251 against 250 shorter 'zzzz game of thrones N' rows (rank -1.878e-06 vs -1.984e-06..-2.062e-06), and the caller's 0–1000 scorer has the same length bias, so no near-match count separates base from fix at the client level — the M2 regression test uses the bare title, the shape the audit probed; the sync skips a write silently (onProgress only, no appLogger line) when the raw body is missing — pre-existing #23 behaviour, noted for a future log line, not fixed here; no new panel calls/probes/retries anywhere (FEWER calls: a stale-disk browse now makes 0 requests when the index is fresh, and one sync instead of refresh+sync when due; 6 direct-fetch call sites removed, 0 new panel-call code added).
- Version: 1.0.10-2.25.0+182. Changelog `about.changelog2274`. UNPROVEN (phone-only): a stale-disk browse refreshes through one sync (no separate re-download); warm search stays 0 panel requests; the exact match survives the cap on the phone; category names update after a refresh without a restart; tap→first-frame time was NOT measured (mock player renders no real first frame — same caveat as #24).

### 27. GitHub Actions release build — the APK is built on GitHub and lands straight on the Release (v1.0.10-2.25.0)
- Why (the owner, 2026-11-11 ~05:26 EST): Codemagic's artifact downloads kept failing — its signed URLs expire in ~2 min and the 345 MB file never survived the transfer to this VM (5 failed browser captures for v1.0.10; direct curl 401; authenticated API calls hang with 0 bytes). The APK file only existed on Codemagic, so no GitHub Release could be created. Building on GitHub Actions puts the APK straight onto the Release the in-app updater reads.
- What: NEW `.github/workflows/android-release.yml`. Trigger: push of a tag `v*`, or manual `workflow_dispatch`. It mirrors `codemagic.yaml` exactly — Flutter 3.47.9, Java 17 (Temurin), `flutter pub get`, `flutter build apk --debug --dart-define=ENABLE_UPDATE_CHECK=true` — on `ubuntu-latest`. The debug signing key is the committed `android/app/debug.keystore` (see `android/app/build.gradle.kts`), so a GitHub-built APK carries the identical certificate and installs over the top of Codemagic-built ones. The APK is renamed to `PlezTV.<tag>.apk` (e.g. `PlezTV.v1.0.10-2.25.0.apk`, matching past release assets), uploaded as a workflow artifact, and on tag pushes attached to a Release created by `softprops/action-gh-release@v2` (`permissions: contents: write`). `workflow_dispatch` builds only (no release).
- How to ship from now on: push a tag `vX.Y.Z-2.25.0` to main (the tag must point at a commit that contains the workflow file); the workflow builds and creates the Release. Codemagic stays as a backup builder — `codemagic.yaml` untouched.
- Version: 1.0.10-2.25.0+182, tag v1.0.10-2.25.0. UNPROVEN until the first run finishes: the Actions run itself, the release-creation step (GITHUB_TOKEN permissions), and the APK download from the Release.

Any screen rendering an M3U/Xtream badge crashed: auth, add-connection, add-plex-account, profile detail/switch, borrow-connection, library-server label, catalog item detail, account preferences.

Regression guard is a DIRECT bundle read — NEW `test/widgets/backend_badge_test.dart` (`rootBundle.load` of all five badge icons) plus widget tests pumping BackendBadge per backend. Lesson (measured): the widget tests passed even with the bug — the SVG load error never reaches the widget test — so a widget test alone is USELESS as a guard for missing assets; the bundle load is the real guard. Failing-first verified: the bundle test fails pre-fix with the exact phone error.
- Copy button (the owner, ~05:31 EST): the Last crash row gains a Copy button — copies `record.describe()` to the clipboard, 'Details copied' snackbar (reuses `t.startup.copyDetails` / `t.startup.detailsCopied` — no new strings).

Test-harness notes (test-only, not product bugs): a bare `Clipboard.getData` await inside fake async hangs forever — the binding installs no clipboard mock, so both Copy tests fake `SystemChannels.platform` (setData stores, getData returns it) and read the clipboard back inside `tester.runAsync`; the history screen's DateFormat needs `setUpAll(() => initializeDateFormatting('en'))` (the app does this at startup in main.dart); the history Copy test needs `pumpAndSettle()` after expanding the tile (the button is offstage until the ExpansionTile animation finishes).
- Version: 1.0.10-2.25.0+182 (same release as §26/§27 — the crash was in the not-yet-released 1.0.10, so no version bump). UNPROVEN (phone-only): opening a screen with an Xtream/M3U badge no longer crashes; Last crash row Copy/History work; the history page lists past crashes with dates and per-crash Copy works.

### 29. People search skipped for IPTV + sync dialog reports real failures (v1.0.10-2.25.0, versionCode 182)
- Why (the owner, 2026-11-11 ~05:50 EST, v1.0.9 logs + screenshots): "It looks like people search is in the way for iptv" — and a manual "Sync catalog now" showed Live/Movies/Series all "failed" under a summary reading "Catalog synced".
- People search: `DataAggregationService.searchAcrossServers` ran `_searchPeopleBestEffort` for every server, and `IptvClient.searchPeople` throws `UnsupportedError` on every call (Xtream/M3U carry no cast or crew) — a warning + full stack trace in the log on every keystroke search. The throw is instant, so it was log spam, not latency. Fix: `_searchPeopleBestEffort` early-returns `[]` when `client.backend` is M3U or Xtream (same backend check the titles leg already uses). `MediaServerClient.searchPeople` has exactly one caller. Test: the fake `_LibrariesClient` in `test/services/data_aggregation_bridge_test.dart` gains a `backend` param (default plex) + a `peopleCalled` flag; new test registers an xtream fake whose searchPeople throws and asserts it is never called, people stay empty, titles still land. (The title row in that test is a Plex-shaped item — the helper has no IPTV shape — and the skip keys off the CLIENT's backend, not the row's.)
- Sync dialog: `_syncSearchIndex` records a per-type fetch failure in the returned `IptvSearchSyncResult.skipped` map ('failed'/'oversize'/'empty-response'/'partial') and returns NORMALLY — it only throws on a client-level failure. The dialog's `_run()` only set `_failed` in the catch, so every per-type failure still read "Catalog synced". Fix: after the await, `failed = true` when any `result.skipped` reason is not 'up-to-date'. The per-row "failed" rendering was already correct (progress event `phase: 'skipped', reason: 'failed'` → `_phaseText` returns the reason). Test: NEW widget test in `test/screens/iptv/iptv_screens_test.dart` — pumps the real `IptvSettingsScreen` with a real `IptvClient` whose `_FakeXtreamApi` has `failCatalogs = true` (every catalog + category fetch throws the connection-timeout the panel produced on the owner's phone), taps "Sync catalog now", and asserts the summary reads "Catalog sync failed" (not "Catalog synced") with all three rows reading "failed". Harness notes (test-only): the file-level setUp switches the disk cache off (`IptvCatalogDiskCache.debugDisableDisk = true`) — with it on, `_searchIndex()` returns null and the sync takes the index-null short-circuit (all-'failed', NO progress events, rows stay spinners); this test re-enables it and points `IptvSearchIndex.debugDirectoryProvider` at a temp dir so the per-type fetch path actually runs; the dialog opens from "Sync catalog now" — "Refresh playlists" is a different row (clears the playlist cache + EPG, shows a snackbar) and tapping it silently tests nothing.
- Failing-first verified for both (product code stashed, both new tests fail on base, pass on the fix). Bridge file 42/42; screens file 13/13 (both files together 55/55). Analyze 49 = baseline.
- Same release as §26/§27/§28 — no version bump. UNPROVEN (phone-only): no more "People search failed" warnings in the log on IPTV searches; a failed manual refresh says "Catalog sync failed".
- NOT in this build (reported to the owner, proposed next): IPTV search items carry no `libraryGlobalKey`, so a SCOPED search (from inside a library) drops every IPTV result — the likely cause of his 49-second search returning 0; and the series index stays empty while the panel times out, so series searches fall back to the 50-second panel sweep (R2 territory).

## 51. One-parse sync — catalog refresh streams to a file, one isolate parse (v1.2.10-2.25.0, versionCode 204)

- Why: research item #2 in `RESEARCH_LOWEND_EFFICIENCY.md` (§5), the biggest remaining transient in the sync: per type the sync buffered the whole body as a String on main, parsed it in an isolate for the full list, then `compute()`d the body to the index job (2nd port copy, re-parsed), then re-encoded the visible body off-thread (3rd copy, re-parsed), then filtered into a visible list — the VOD body parsed THREE times, copied twice, while main held body String + full parsed list + visible list. On a 1.5 GB TV that transient is plausibly 400-700 MB and the likeliest LMK-kill window after the bind was fixed in 1.2.6.
- 51.1 (stream to file) — NEW `XtreamApi._fetchBodyToFile` + public `getLiveStreamsToFile` / `getVodStreamsToFile` / `getSeriesToFile` (`lib/services/iptv/xtream_api.dart`): the response streams to a file (same `AbortableRequest` + User-Agent, status checks, refusal recording as `_fetchBody`; per-chunk-gap timeout like `getXmltvFeed`; oversize cap enforced on streamed BYTES vs `maxCatalogResponseChars`; partial file deleted on any failure). Full-list only; returns the file path; does NOT set `last*StreamsBody` and does NOT parse. The old `get*Streams` + `last*StreamsBody` are unchanged — the disk-load / first-load fallback writes (iptv_client.dart:2386, 2517, 2660, 5609) still read the raw body from them.
- 51.2 (one isolate job) — `writeIndexTypeInBackground` (`lib/services/iptv/iptv_search_index.dart`) now takes `IndexWriteParams.visibleIndexed` + `diskFilePath` and returns `IndexWriteResult.visibleRowsJson` + `fetchedCount` + `diskWriteFailed`: the same job parses once (from the file path — the seed path has done file paths since 1.2.6), runs the row-count partial guard itself (visible < half the indexed visible rows keeps the old rows, same 'partial-kept' reason and same guard order: empty, then row-count, then no-categories, then category-coverage), writes the index, writes the visible-only disk file after a COMPLETE land only (same `toJson` payload the old `encode*BodyVisible` round trip produced; a disk-write failure is flagged on the result, never thrown), and returns the visible rows' JSON for the memo publish. `_rowsFromDecoded` also returns the typed rows beside the index rows so the isolate builds the visible JSON without re-parsing (1:1 order). Partial lands stay index-only (series fix, v1.0.16); guard refusals write nothing. The seed path passes neither new param and behaves exactly as before. NEW `IptvCatalogDiskCache.writeFilePath` gives the isolate its destination (null when disk is disabled).
- 51.3 (sync rewire) — `_syncSearchIndex` (`lib/media/iptv_client.dart`): per type, categories as before, then the catalog streams to a temp file in one `plezy_sync_` temp dir per run (deleted in the finally; fetch-failure paths keep the file until the run ends, then the dir goes). Empty = zero-byte file (checked on main, no isolate round trip); the partial guard lives in the isolate now; 'writing' progress is emitted without a row count. After compute, the category file is still written on main, the disk-write-failure flag is logged, and the memo publish rebuilds typed rows from `result.visibleRowsJson` via per-type `fromJson`. `syncSearchIndexForBackground` (R4) routes through the same engine with no separate change. DELETED `_visibleFetchedRows` (its two call sites — the guard and the publish — both moved into the isolate); `_visibleRows` stays for the first-load fallback paths.
- 51.4 (tests) — NEW `test/services/iptv/iptv_one_parse_sync_test.dart` (S1-S7): S1 VOD sync never holds the body on main (`last*StreamsBody` null) and lands visible rows in index + disk + memos; S2 live + series same, memo publish via `iptvChannels()`; S3 partial-kept guard preserved (warm partial series keeps 12 rows); S4 empty-response guard preserved; S5 the R4 background path also never holds the body (stale-warm setup); S6 Trex-scale VOD sync (199,762 rows, 3 of 4 categories hidden, 49,941 visible land); S7 `getVodStreamsToFile` writes the body to a file without setting `lastVodStreamsBody`. FAILING-FIRST: S1, S2, S5, S6 failed on the 1.2.9 base (S1 with the body held on main); S3/S4 passed on base (guard regression guards); S7 could not be written failing-first (the getter did not exist on base). Test fakes updated with to-file overrides: `_BenchmarkFakeXtreamApi` (build13, counter moved to the to-file getter), `_OversizeSeriesApi` (build23, oversize throw moved), `_RepairFakeApi` (build19), `_RegionFakeApi` (region-groups probes), `R2FakeXtreamApi` (shared helper, same counters + failure behaviour). NO RSS BOUND (measured 2026-11-14, honest negative): the peak RSS sampled during a Trex-scale VOD sync is 256-295MB on the fix vs 255-270MB on the base — indistinguishable in this harness (the process peak is dominated by the isolate's single parse heap in both cases, plus the test's own in-process fixture; the 50ms sampler misses the short-lived main-side copies the fix removes). A bound here would pass on the base or flake, so S6 prints the deltas for the record and asserts only the deterministic proof (null body + identical lands). The structural saving stands: main never holds the body String + full parsed list + visible list at once, and the body is parsed once, not three times.
- Version: 1.2.10-2.25.0+204. Changelog `about.changelog2296` (4 entries). UNPROVEN (phone-only): a real Trex-scale sync's RSS on the Fold / a low-end TV (the harness cannot measure the saving — see 51.4); the temp dir cleaning up on a sync killed mid-run (the finally covers a completed run; a process kill leaves the dir to the OS); About 1.2.10-2.25.0; the updater offering it and not re-offering after install.

## 52. Disk-paged browse — browse, hubs and the guide read pages from the search index (v1.2.11-2.25.0, versionCode 205)

- Why: research item #3 in `RESEARCH_LOWEND_EFFICIENCY.md` (§5), the highest-risk item in the plan: the three memoized catalog lists held all 302,114 rows (~90-150 MB steady-state) just so browse/guide/hubs could slice them. The search index already held the same rows on disk — browse now pages from it instead, and the memos disappear for those paths.
- 52.1 (index, schema v3) — `lib/services/iptv/iptv_search_index.dart`: NEW columns `added` (VOD unix seconds) / `release_date` (series ISO date) / `seq` (provider-order position within connection+type) on `iptv_search_item`, plus browse indexes `(connection_id, type, category_id, seq)` / `(connection_id, type, added)` / `(connection_id, type, release_date)`; `_schemaVersion` 2 → 3 (the existing drop-and-rebuild migration; the seed re-fills from disk). `IptvSearchIndexRow` carries the three fields (factories populate them; `fromDb` reads them with the name_norm absent-column fallback; `toVodStream`/`toSeries` restore `added`/`releaseDate`, which the old converters dropped). `_insertRowsLocked` assigns `seq = MAX(seq)+1+i` inside the transaction — a complete re-sync (delete-then-insert, or an upsert whose incoming list covers every kept row) restarts at 0 in provider order; a partial merge continues after the kept rows. NEW `IptvBrowseOrder` (provider/added/releaseDate) + `listByCategory` (paged, ORDER BY seq / added DESC NULLS LAST / release_date DESC NULLS LAST, ties by seq) + `countByCategory` (same filter, COUNT) + `countByCategoryGrouped` (one grouped query for the categories screen). The browse search/initial filters run on a SQL CASE mirroring `IptvClient.stripCategoryPrefix` exactly (leading `<categoryName> - `, case-insensitive, only when the title is longer than the prefix) — `name_norm` is untouched, so search (R2.2) cannot regress; the hidden sets filter at read time by id or category initial, null-category rows never hidden (same rule as `visibleRowCount`).
- 52.2 (client, index-first) — `lib/media/iptv_client.dart`: NEW `_warmIndexForType` (index + rows > 0, else the caller falls back to the memo path), `_indexBrowsePage` (rows + total, fires the existing index due check so a stale index still re-syncs), `_indexAllRows` (bounded 5000-row pages for the guide / full-list hub detail — transient to the call, never a held memo), `debugAnyMemoLoaded` test seam. `fetchLibraryPagedContent`, `fetchLibraryHubs`, `fetchGlobalHubs`, `fetchMoreHubItemsPage`, `_moviesItems`/`_seriesItems`, `iptvItemsForCategory`, `iptvCategoriesForLibrary` (grouped counts + the index's category table, in panel order — `categoryNames` now `ORDER BY rowid`), and the guide all read the index first and fall back to the memo path when cold. `fetchItem`/`fetchChildren` resolve from the index first (point lookup), memo second. `_vodCategoryNames`/`_seriesCategoryNames`/`_liveCategoryNames` prefer the index's category table when warm. `unhiddenCategoriesNeedRefresh` reads grouped index counts when all three types are warm (the count is about STORAGE, so no hidden filter is applied — a still-hidden category with rows stored reads as no-refresh-needed); a cold type falls back to the memo path. The toggle re-derive (`_rederiveMemosFromDisk`) now re-derives only memos that are loaded or loading, and does nothing when none is loaded.
- 52.3 (bind) — `warmUpCatalogsSequentially` now seeds one type at a time (live → first frame → RSS gate → VOD → series), same phase-log names/order (`live`, `first-frame`, `vod`, `series`, `deferred-memory`); the public `warmUp*Catalog` wrappers seed only (no memo load). `_seedSearchIndexFromDisk`'s per-type body is extracted as `_seedSearchIndexTypeFromDisk(type, {force, refreshState})`, which returns the isolate result and refreshes the index state when asked (the loop refreshes once; direct callers refresh per type — the bind's state previously only refreshed via the post-bind seed, which no longer exists as a separate step).
- 52.4 (Reload) — the research doc's one-line "Reload override flag" did not survive contact with the code: Reload publishes a fresh live memo but does not write the index, so a once-only flag would serve stale index rows on the guide's second call (the guide rebuilds after Reload's notify). Instead Reload (a) stamps `_liveReloadStamp` and the guide serves the fresh memo while the index is stale relative to it (`_liveMemoFresherThanIndex`, cleared when a later sync stamps past it), and (b) force re-seeds live from the disk file Reload just wrote, dropping the memo + the flag when the re-seed lands. When the warm index refuses that re-seed (a visible-only disk file covering under half the categories reads as partial), the flag stays and the memo keeps serving until the next sync heals the index — the guide stays right either way. The guide's playlist-change fingerprint is skipped on repeat calls over an unchanged index (lastSync + row count), the way the memo path skipped it on a memo hit.
- 52.5 (tests) — NEW `test/services/iptv/iptv_disk_paged_browse_test.dart` (A1-A7 index API + B1-B5 client): A1 provider-order paging stitches with no gaps/dupes; A2 counts agree with the memo count; A3 search + initial match the memo filter exactly (`alice` → [102], `2011` → [103], `NF` → [], `A` → [102, 104], `#` → [105]); A4 hidden by id/initial excluded in list and count, null-category row never hidden; A5 added/release_date newest-first, nulls last; A6 v2 DB migrates to v3 and re-seeds from disk with sort columns populated; A7 a complete re-sync (upsert-merge, the production path) restores exact provider order after a partial land. B1 warm index + deleted disk catalog files → browse/hubs/guide make ZERO panel requests (counting fake panel) and no memo loads; B2 index-path items identical to the memo path page by page (browse, search, initial, guide, drill-down, counts, hubs); B3 the un-hide check reads the index (pruned → refresh needed; stored → not); B4 Trex-scale VOD (199,762 rows, 3 of 4 categories hidden, 49,941 visible) stitches in 1000-row pages with zero requests; B5 cold index falls back to the memo path. FAILING-FIRST: the file failed to compile on the 1.2.10 base (listByCategory/countByCategory/IptvBrowseOrder/debugAnyMemoLoaded do not exist), and a scratch test on base proved the B1 scenario makes 2 panel requests there (`get_vod_streams: 1, get_live_streams: 1`). UPDATED `test/services/iptv/iptv_catalog_bind_1_2_6_test.dart` M1 for the new bind shape (index holds visible rows; search answers from the index with `loadSource: 'index'`; no memo loaded by the bind or by search). Full iptv suite: 618 passed / 1 failed — the single failure is the known build13 Trex-scale latency bench (B2), which passes standalone on the fix (9/9 for the file) and on base (9/9), i.e. the documented in-suite timing flake, not a regression. NO RSS BOUND (same honest-negative rule as 1.2.10): the deterministic proof is the zero-panel-request assertion + identical lands; an RSS number was not measured in-harness.
- Version: 1.2.11-2.25.0+205. Changelog `about.changelog2297` (4 entries). UNPROVEN (phone-only): the memo heap actually gone on the Fold / a low-end TV (the harness proves the paths, not the device RSS); About 1.2.11-2.25.0; the updater offering it and not re-offering after install; Reload's re-seed landing on the real panel (it is refused when over half the live categories are hidden — the memo then serves until the next sync).


## 53. EPG windowed + lazy feed load — the guide keeps a window, not the whole feed (v1.2.12-2.25.0, versionCode 206)

- Why: research item #4 in `RESEARCH_LOWEND_EFFICIENCY.md` (§5, §602): the guide retained EVERY in-window programme of the whole feed (~267k objects, ~50-60 MB at Trex scale) plus six derived index maps, and the feed load had no gate — with the startup section on Live TV the guide mounted at startup and triggered a full feed download + parse within seconds of the bind. The research doc's one-line "gate it too" did not survive contact with the code in one respect: the gate cannot be a plain "wait for short-EPG" latch, because the per-channel fetch calls short-EPG first and only then the feed — a gate that opened on ANY short-EPG settle would open on the first row's answer, before the other visible rows answered. The gate as built opens on (1) the in-flight short-EPG count dropping to 0 after a round settled, non-low-end only; (2) the user scrolling past the first screen; (3) a 60 s safety timeout. The other wrinkle the doc did not call out: a window slide must be triggered by the TRIGGERING channel's own candidates only, not by a neighbour missing from the window — otherwise every scrolled row extends the window by one and re-parses the feed (at Trex scale, once per row instead of once per ~50 rows). The window test W5 caught that during development (672 retained instead of 624) and the ensure was fixed before shipping.
- 53.1 (guide shape) — `lib/media/iptv_live_tv.dart`, `_CachedXmltvGuide` rewritten: the `programs` list and the six programme-derived index maps (`byChannel`, `byNormalizedName`, `byDisplayName`, `byChannelFolded`, `byNumericId`, `byLooseName`) are gone. What remains is metadata for the whole feed — `displayNames`, `channelIdsSeen`, `programsParsed`, `channelsParsed`, `programCountByChannel` (per-id counts, post-dedupe), `programSlotsByChannel` (a 30-minute slot bitmap per id over the I8 parse window, ~90 bytes/channel — the overlap test is exact at slot granularity, so `hasProgramsInWindow` answers exactly what mapping the rows would have answered), `slotOriginEpoch`, `totalProgramCount` — plus `windowByChannel`, the rows of the window feed ids only, capped at `maxWindowFeedIds` = 64 by wholesale replacement per slide (no LRU). The metadata-only indexes (`foldedChannelIds`, `numericChannelIds`, `displayNameChannels`, `normalizedNameChannels`, `looseNameChannels`, `mixedChannelIds`, `looseMixedChannelIds`, `nameCandidatesFor`, `looseCandidatesFor`) are derived exactly as the old programme-derived ones were (id-side indexes from the ids WITH programmes — `programCountByChannel` keys — name-side from `displayNames`), so matching behaviour is unchanged; the fold is `toLowerCase()`, the name key is `normalizeEpgChannelName`, the loose key is `looseEpgChannelNameKey` (the first draft used `epgFold`/`normalizeEpgDisplayName`/`normalizeEpgChannelId`/`looseNormalize`, which do not exist — caught by `dart analyze`, fixed to the old implementations). Dedupe by (channel, start), first-wins, is unchanged (a transient Set in the new `_XmltvGuideAccumulator`, the old constructor's `_dedupePrograms`); counts are post-dedupe. The 304 path reuses the guide via the new `withFetchedAt` copy (metadata + window unchanged). The F2 nearest-outside-window diagnostic now reads the slot bitmap (`nearestProgramStart`, 30-minute granularity) instead of the rows.
- 53.2 (load path) — both parse sites (`_loadXmltvGuide` download parse, `_readPersistedXmltv` warm-start parse) take a `retainIds` set and build the guide in ONE pass through `_XmltvGuideAccumulator` (counts, bitmaps, window rows — the whole feed is never held as a list, as before). Threaded through `_startXmltvDownload` → `_xmltvGuideForKey` → `_loadSourceGuide`. Bulk loads (Reload, update-now, Assign, overview via `_loadEnabledFeeds`) pass empty retainIds = metadata only, no rows. The per-channel fetch passes the window (`_windowFeedIdsFor`: the triggering channel's candidates + guide-order neighbours' ±25 positions, capped at 64 with the triggering channel first). A background refresh (stale-while-revalidate) keeps the CURRENT guide's window ids, so a refresh never empties the window the guide is showing. A full Trex-scale stream-parse takes ~25 s for 960,000 programmes / ~151 MB (measured this session in `iptv_epg_efficiency_test.dart`, `parseMs=25388`) — that measurement killed any design that re-parses per channel or per scroll step; a slide costs one 25 s parse, the same as today's first-miss stall, amortized over ~50 rows. No background prefetch in this version (deliberately deferred as a follow-up).
- 53.3 (match) — `_matchInGuide` gains a `loadRows` flag (default true). With `loadRows` true (the per-channel fetch path, after `_ensureGuideWindow`) the tiers map the window rows exactly as before; with `loadRows` false (Reload, `channelsWithoutGuide`, `_channelHasFeedMatch`) the tiers use `hasProgramsInWindow` (bitmap) + `hasRetainedPrograms` (count > 0) and carry no programmes (bulk callers never read them) — the match decision is identical either way. `_resolveChannelAcrossFeeds` threads the flag. `_probeLocalMatch` (detail sheet, reads `resolution.programs.length`) ensures the window first, `loadRows: true`. `_windowCandidateIds` computes one channel's matchable feed ids from metadata only (mapping id, tvg-id candidates incl. folded + numeric, name + loose candidates); `_ensureGuideWindow` slides only when the triggering channel's OWN candidates have retained programmes but no rows, re-parsing the persisted file (`_slideGuideWindow`, fetchedAt preserved — same bytes, same generation) or re-downloading when there is no persisted file. `guide.programs.length` / `byChannel` reads are replaced everywhere: `_recordSourceSuccess` → `totalProgramCount` + `programCountByChannel.length`; Reload `feedSummary` / `feedChannelIds` / `feedSamples` / F2 nearest; `updateEpgNow` feed outcomes; `epgFeedChannels` (Assign picker) → `programCountByChannel` entries; `_lastXmltvFeedChannelIds` → `programCountByChannel.length`.
- 53.4 (feed gate) — `_fetchFeedPrograms` checks the gate first (Xtream only — M3U has no short-EPG, its feed IS the first source): while closed it returns `feedDeferred: true` immediately (the D1b deferral — `_recordFeedDeferral` + the idle listener bumps `epgVersion` so rows re-fetch when the gate opens) and starts the safety timer (`debugFeedGateSafetyTimeout`, 60 s, test-settable). `_openFeedGate()` is a one-way latch (also bumps `_epgVersion` when deferred channels exist); the timer is cancelled in `cancelBackgroundWork`. The gate opens on (1) `_releaseShortEpgSlot` when the in-flight short-EPG count drops to 0 AND `!DevicePerformance.isLowEndHardware`; (2) `noteGuideScrolledPastFirstScreen()` (public, called by the guide tab); (3) the safety timeout. `bypassFeedGate` is threaded from `fetchEpgForChannel` → `_fetchXtreamEpg`/`_fetchM3uEpg` → `_fetchXtreamEpgWithAbort` → `_fetchFeedPrograms`/`_mergeFeedTail`; the detail sheet refresh and the player live-scrub pass `true` (one explicitly-opened channel); the guide tab never does. Bulk paths (`_loadEnabledFeeds`) never go through `_fetchFeedPrograms`, so they are ungated by construction.
- 53.5 (cache cap) — `_epgCache` is capped at 64 entries (`_maxEpgCacheEntries`, LRU by insertion order via the new `_putEpgCache` helper, which routes ALL seven write sites); eviction loses nothing — the persisted per-channel file is the backing store.
- 53.6 (guide tab) — `lib/screens/livetv/tabs/guide_tab.dart`: `_channelVerticalController` gains a listener in `initState` (removed in `dispose`) that calls `support.noteGuideScrolledPastFirstScreen()` once when scrolled more than one viewport height. No widget test for the hook (the guide tab is heavy to pump); support-level tests + code review instead.
- 53.7 (tests) — NEW `test/services/iptv/iptv_epg_windowed_lazy_test.dart` (12 tests, 100 channels x 24 programmes fixture): W1 short-EPG answers → no feed parse, no download; W2 short-EPG misses → lazy feed load, programmes identical to the fixture, retained ≤ 624 (window) < 2400 (whole feed); W3 scroll to a far channel → window slides, programmes correct, retained stays 624, exactly one re-parse, no download; W4 cold guide open on low-end with short-EPG hanging → no feed parse/download, gate closed; scroll opens the gate → feed loads; W4b safety timeout opens the gate on its own; W5 three visible rows share one window (exactly 624 retained, one parse); W6 middle-channel programmes identical to the eager path (titles + start times); W7 Reload reports the same counts (withGuide 100, feedChannelIds 100, tvgId tier 100, samples 24 programmes each) with 0 retained rows; W8 channels-without-guide is the same set (feed covers 50 of 100 → exactly those 50 keys) with 0 retained rows; W9 the per-channel cache caps at 64 after 100 fetches and an evicted channel still serves; W10 the Assign picker lists all 100 feed channels + counts + display names from metadata with 0 retained rows; W11 on low-end short-EPG settling does NOT open the gate, scrolling does. FAILING-FIRST: the file does not compile on the 1.2.11 base (the new debug hooks + `noteGuideScrolledPastFirstScreen` do not exist), and a scratch test (base APIs only, deleted after the proof) PASSED on base — cold guide open with hanging short-EPG serves the stale feed and refresh-downloads it (`xmltvCalls` 1) — and FAILED on the fix (gate defers: programmes empty, no download). New tests: 12/12 pass. Full iptv suite: 630 passed / 1 failed — the single failure is the known build13 Trex-scale latency bench (first launch), which passes standalone on the fix 2 of 3 re-runs (6/6, 5/6 with a 3479 ms tick gap, 6/6) and 6/6 on base once, i.e. the documented in-suite timing flake, not a regression. `flutter analyze`: 53 = base 53. Targeted screens (livetv except the three known-hang mini-player files + the EPG settings screens): 108/108 pass. The FULL screens suite did NOT complete: the run was killed by the 3000 s timeout at 49:27 (+835 ~3 −15 at cutoff, runner died mid-load), so its 15 failures at cutoff are unattributed — the targeted run above is the coverage for this change's widget surface. NO RSS BOUND (same honest-negative rule as 1.2.10/1.2.11): the deterministic proof is the retained-programme bound (624 vs 2400 on the fixture) + the zero-parse gate assertions; an RSS number was not measured in-harness.
- Version: 1.2.12-2.25.0+206. Changelog `about.changelog2298` (4 entries). UNPROVEN (phone-only): retained EPG actually ~5-10 MB on the Fold / a low-end TV (the harness proves the window bound, not the device RSS); the gate opening on a real guide open (short-EPG settle on a non-low-end phone, scroll on a low-end TV); a window slide on the real panel (one re-parse, no re-download); About 1.2.12-2.25.0; the updater offering it and not re-offering after install.

## 54. Emergency debug log saver — after 3 failed starts the app opens a saver instead of silently dumping and starting normally (v1.2.13-2.25.0, versionCode 207)

- Why: the owner (2026-11-14 ~17:21 EST), on the 1.2.9 auto-dump: the silent write assumed saving a dump needs no permission, and he realised recording/saving might need permissions — so instead of just auto-dumping, after 3 crashes the app should do a DIFFERENT startup, an "auto dump startup", that kills all normal startup processes and asks for all permissions needed to record the dump and where to save it. The permission half was verified, not assumed (below): NO permission is needed for either half, so the screen asks WHERE to save, not for a permission — and the safe-mode screen is still the right shape, because a crashing TV now stops and asks instead of crashing a fourth time.
- 54.1 (permissions, verified) — (a) RECORDING: the dump reads only in-app data — the app's own rolling log file, settings, memory numbers, the last crash record (`ActivityManager.getHistoricalProcessExitReasons` for its own exits needs no permission either) — no logcat, no READ_LOGS, no permission. (b) SAVING: the system save picker (Storage Access Framework, ACTION_CREATE_DOCUMENT) needs no storage permission — Android Developers, "Access documents and other files": "Because the user is involved in selecting the files or directories that your app can access, this mechanism doesn't require any system permissions" (available from API 19); the app-specific external files dir (`getExternalFilesDir`) is scoped-storage exempt on ALL API levels, no permission; `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` is a no-op on API 29+ and grants nothing extra on API 30+. `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (all-files access) is NOT requested — it is restricted to file managers/backup tools and the picker makes it unnecessary. The screen carries the one-line caption "No special permission is needed — choose where to save the file, or save it in the app folder."
- 54.2 (routing) — `lib/main.dart`: `_bootstrapApp` is now `async` and AWAITS the counter before deciding (two platform-channel round trips, negligible against a startup about to load the catalog — the branch must not race the normal startup). `_recordLaunchAndMaybeDump` is renamed `_recordLaunchAndCountFailedStarts` and returns the count; it NO LONGER writes the dump at the threshold (the 1.2.9 silent write is gone — the saver owns the dump now, only after the user asks for it). NEW `shouldEnterAutoDumpSafeMode(int)` → `failedStarts >= AutoDumpService.failedStartThreshold` (3, unchanged). At the threshold `_runAutoDumpSafeMode` runs a minimal `MaterialApp` (TranslationProvider + InputModeTracker, same wrappers as the bootstrap; system theme via `monoTheme`) with `EmergencyDebugLogSaverScreen` as home; otherwise `_runNormalStartup` (the old body: exit observer + version prime + `runApp(StartupBootstrap(...))`). Safe mode installs NO `_AppExitObserver` — closing the saver without saving or skipping writes no clean exit, so the counter stays at 3 and the saver shows again next launch. `onContinueStartup` re-enters `_runNormalStartup` in-process (runApp twice is standard Flutter). The picker is hidden on Android TV (`showSavePicker: !(Platform.isAndroid && PlatformDetector.isTV())`) — the primary save button writes straight to the app folder there.
- 54.3 (screen) — NEW `lib/screens/auto_dump_safe_mode_screen.dart`, `EmergencyDebugLogSaverScreen` (stateful; `_savedPath` null → pre-save, non-null → post-save). Exact copy (the owner's official wording, 2026-11-14 ~17:24 EST): title "Emergency Debug Log Saver"; body "PlezTV has failed to start several times in a row. You can save an emergency debug log and send it to us, or continue starting up as usual."; buttons "Save debug log" / "Continue startup". Pre-save: primary "Save debug log" (picker when `showSavePicker`, else straight to the app folder) + "Save in the app folder" (only when the picker is available) + "Continue startup" (resets the counter, proceeds to normal startup) + the no-permission caption; a picker failure (not a cancel) shows "Could not save the debug log — try saving it in the app folder instead." and both save buttons stay on screen — never silently fails to save; a cancel changes nothing. Post-save: "Saved to: <path>" (selectable, monospace) + Share (`SharePlus.instance.share`, same call shape as the logs screen, failure → `t.messages.autoDumpShareFailed` snackbar) / "Restart PlezTV" (proceeds to normal startup; the counter was already reset by the save) / Close (`AppExitService.requestExit`, injectable seam, same pattern as `StartupFailureView`).
- 54.4 (service) — `lib/services/auto_dump_service.dart`: `writeDump`'s dump-text build is extracted as NEW `buildDumpText({failedStarts, dumpReason})` (reason defaults to the 1.2.9 `'auto-dump after N failed starts'` — the T-A5 test asserts it); NEW `writeDumpToPicker({failedStarts})` → `({String? path, bool cancelled})`: builds the SAME summary-first text (`buildDebugText` — summary, then the FULL rolling log, then Settings / Memory / Last crash, fully redacted) with reason `'emergency debug log saver after N failed starts'`, calls `FilePickerService.instance.saveFile` (dialog title reuses `t.logs.exportDebugDialogTitle` = "Save debug file", fileName fixed `pleztv-autodump.txt`, `FileType.custom`, `['txt']`), resets the counter on success, never throws (null + cancelled=false = failed). The existing app-specific-dir write (`writeDump`) is now the "Save in the app folder" fallback; the Logs screen's "Last auto-dump" row keeps working with it (it only sees the app-folder file, not a picker-chosen one).
- 54.5 (tests) — UPDATED `test/services/auto_dump_test.dart` T-A1 + `failedLaunch()` helper to the new contract (threshold → NO dump file, counter stays 3, `shouldEnterAutoDumpSafeMode(3)` true); T-A5 untouched. NEW `test/services/auto_dump_startup_test.dart` (S1-S5 service + W1-W6 widget): S1 predicate (3 → true, 2 → false); S2 threshold launch writes NO dump + counter stays 3; S3 picker save with a fake delegate writes the summary-first dump + counter reset; S4 picker cancelled → no file, counter stays 3; S5 picker throws → failed, no file, counter stays 3; W1 pump at 3 → exact title/body/buttons, `find.byKey(startupBootstrapProgressKey)` finds nothing (no normal bootstrap — no bind is structural: the safe-mode tree never constructs IptvClient/MultiServerManager); W2 tap "Save debug log" with a fake picker → confirmation + Share, tap Share → fake `SharePlatform.instance` receives the file; W3 "Save in the app folder" → `pleztv-autodump.txt` in the external override dir + counter reset; W4 "Continue startup" → counter 0 + `onContinueStartup` called; W5 pump + dispose without saving/skipping → counter stays 3; W6 TV mode (`showSavePicker=false`) → picker never called, app-folder button hidden, "Save debug log" saves to the app folder directly. FAILING-FIRST (stash proof): the new file does not compile on the 1.2.12 base (`shouldEnterAutoDumpSafeMode` / `writeDumpToPicker` / `emergencyDebugLogSaver` do not exist). The behavioural flip — production no longer writes at the threshold — is proven statically (`writeDump` referenced once in base `main.dart`, zero times on the fix) plus S2/T-A1 passing on the fix; the OLD T-A1 still passes on the fix only because its helper calls `writeDump` itself (it tests the service, not production routing). New tests: 11/11 pass; `auto_dump_test.dart`: 15/15 pass. Full iptv suite: 630 passed / 1 failed — the single failure is the known build13 Trex-scale latency bench (first launch), which passes standalone on the fix 6/6, i.e. the documented in-suite timing flake, not a regression. `flutter analyze`: 53 = base 53 (none in the changed files). NO RSS BOUND (same honest-negative rule as 1.2.10/1.2.11/1.2.12): no memory number was measured in-harness for this change. Widget-test note: the save chain does real async disk I/O (`file.exists()` / `readAsString()` / `writeAsBytes()`), which never completes in the fake-async zone — the tests use 12 × [runAsync 200 ms + pump] rounds, one per real await; 8 rounds were one short (file landed at round 4, full completion at round 7–8, and the tap consumes a round before the chain starts).
- Version: 1.2.13-2.25.0+207. Changelog `about.changelog2299` (4 entries). UNPROVEN (phone-only): the saver appearing on the real TV after 3 failed starts; the system save picker on the Fold writing a real file; Share from the saver; "Continue startup" landing on the main screen; About 1.2.13-2.25.0; the updater offering it and not re-offering after install.
## 55. EPG display/gate fixes — the guide stops lying about coverage (v1.2.14-2.25.0, versionCode 208)

- 55.1 (G1 feed-gate fix) — `lib/media/iptv_live_tv.dart`: the 1.2.12 gate made a cold guide open look emptier — while closed, `_fetchFeedPrograms` returned `feedDeferred` and the row rendered "No information available" placeholders for a feed never consulted, for up to 60 s on low-end TV unless the user scrolled. The gate now exists ONLY on low-end hardware (`DevicePerformance.isLowEndHardware`) and opens on the FIRST short-EPG miss: reaching `_fetchFeedPrograms` means a row's short-EPG already missed (every Xtream caller has a short-EPG result first), so there is nothing to defer — the feed loads on that very miss. On non-low-end hardware there is no gate at all. The gate's memory purpose survives on low-end: a guide open where short-EPG answers every visible row never downloads the feed at all. The safety-timer machinery is gone (`_epgFeedGateTimer`, `_startFeedGateSafetyTimer`, `debugFeedGateSafetyTimeout` — the 60 s timeout was part of the bug). `_openFeedGate()` is now a flag flip only: post-G1 there are no gate-deferred channels to wake, so it no longer clears `_deferredEpgChannels` or bumps `_epgVersion` (the version bump would cause one spurious re-fetch round on the first miss; playback-deferred channels are still healed by `_onPlaybackActivityChanged`). The short-EPG settle open (`_releaseShortEpgSlot`, non-low-end only) and the scroll open (`noteGuideScrolledPastFirstScreen`) are kept as harmless redundant paths. The D1b playback deferral is untouched (separate mechanism, by design).
- 55.2 (G2 blank-row fix) — `lib/screens/livetv/tabs/guide_tab.dart`: `_resetIptvEpgState()` now also clears `_programsByChannelScope` (`= const {}` — the field is `const {}` at init, so it is reassigned, never `.clear()`ed). Without it a row that had programmes from an earlier window kept them while its refetch was in flight, so `_getProgramsForChannel` returned the STALE list without placeholders and the stale programmes all clamped out of the new grid window — a bare background (no spinner, no text, no blocks), as pixel-measured on the FOX/PBS rows. Clearing it makes in-flight rows fall into the designed loading branch (spinner + "Loading guide…"). `_programs` (flat list) is NOT cleared — it is not used for row rendering (only focus identity) and self-heals per channel. The version-change branch in `_ensureIptvEpg` is NOT cleared either — same window there, so stale programmes render correctly as stale-while-revalidate.
- 55.3 (G4 name-key micro-fix) — `lib/media/iptv_live_tv.dart`: NEW `_epgNameGenericWords = {'channel', 'tv'}`, dropped as whole words in `_epgNameWords` (both strict and loose keys), mirroring `_epgIdentityDrop` (which drops the same two words, plus 'bk'). "FOX NEWS" now meets a feed entry called "FOX NEWS CHANNEL". `TV5` is one word and survives; `TV 5` reduces to `5` and still needs the country gate + uniqueness. Gain is tens at most — no number claimed until a fixture proves it. Two existing tests changed with it: the F4 key expectations (`CHANNEL 007` is now `7`, not `channel7`) and the F6A foil display (`FOX Channel` → `FOX Network` — the old foil now normalizes to `fox` too, so the exact-preference would find two); the F6A code comment is updated the same way.
- 55.4 (G5 DROPPED) — the round-3 doc called the F6 ambiguity relaxations (display-name-exact preference; same-channel duplicate collapse only with exactly one in-window holder) unshipped, but they already shipped in 1.2.8 (FORK §49.6 — code in `_matchInGuide`, tests F6A/F6B + the 1.2.5 uniqueness guard; ambiguous moved 249 → 228 because of it). Nothing to build.
- 55.5 (tests) — UPDATED `test/services/iptv/iptv_epg_windowed_lazy_test.dart`: W4 rewritten (low-end, hanging short-EPG: the feed loads on the first miss — programmes land, one parse, gate open; no scroll, no 60 s wait) and W4b rewritten (low-end, empty short-EPG: same, no timer to wait for); the safety-timeout hook reset is gone from tearDown; W11 passes unchanged (short-EPG answering still does not open the gate; scroll does; the far channel then loads from the feed). NEW `test/services/iptv/iptv_epg_fixes_1_2_14_test.dart` (2 tests): the G4 key cases (channel/tv drop, TV5 survives, TV 5 → 5, bare TV/CHANNEL → empty, HDNet kept, loose key drops them too) and the end-to-end match (`US - FOX NEWS HD` meets feed `FOX NEWS CHANNEL`, matchMethod `name`). NEW `test/screens/livetv/guide_tab_epg_blank_row_test.dart` (1 widget test): the real GuideTab + real IptvClient + fake Xtream API + a fake Plex server whose fetchSchedule hangs on demand (armed only right before the +2h shift, so the initial load always completes). W1 programmes sit in the grid's first hour (ending a full hour before the +2h window starts, so they clamp out of it entirely); after the shift both rows must show `Loading guide…` (×2) with no `Show A`/`Show B` text, then the W2 programmes land after the stall is released. FAILING-FIRST (by construction, tests not yet run — the 1.2.13 programmer owns the runner): W4/W4b fail on the 1.2.12 base (the gated feed defers: programmes empty, parse 0, gate closed); the G4 key/match tests fail on base (`foxnewschannel` ≠ `foxnews`); the blank-row test fails on base (no `Loading guide…` text — stale W1 programmes clamp out to bare background). `flutter analyze`: 53 = base 53.
- Version: 1.2.14-2.25.0+208. Changelog `about.changelog2300` (4 entries). UNPROVEN (phone-only): a cold guide open on the Fold/TV showing feed programmes without a 60 s wait or a scroll; no bare-background row during a real window shift; About 1.2.14-2.25.0; the updater offering it and not re-offering after install. HONEST CEILING (unchanged): this build fixes the guide LYING about coverage, not the data ceiling — feed matches stay ~3,396 of 26,151 until a bigger feed, manual assignment, or a user-added US community source (G3, Settings → EPG sources).
## 56. Short-EPG slow answers must not become 24h empties + feed-download diagnostics + guide-coverage sample (v1.2.15-2.25.0, versionCode 209)

- Why: round-4 research (`RESEARCH_EPG_MATCHING_ROUND4.md`, §7) — the top bug is R1: a slow short-EPG answer is aborted at the 15 s first-paint timeout and the resulting empty is cached for 24 h, so one slow panel response is a day-long empty row. The visible guide is short-EPG-first; its real coverage was unmeasured (the Reload 3,396 is feed-only). The feed request is whole (`GET xmltv.php`, no subset params) and the matcher drops nothing — TiviMate cannot beat PlezTV on the feed (tvg-id only, caps 2,816 vs PlezTV's 3,396). G1/G2/G4 are 1.2.14's work (§55, not landed yet — this §56 slots after §55 on rebase); G3 (a community/different guide) is dead by the owner's explicit direction ("I'm not asking you to find a different guide").
- 56.1 (R1, late short-EPG) — `lib/media/iptv_live_tv.dart`: `_fetchXtreamShortEpg` returns `(programs, atLimit, timedOut, latePrograms)` — on the first-paint timeout it does NOT abort the 60 s request; the request keeps running and its listings map into `latePrograms` (errors map to `[]`). `fetchEpgForChannel`: a `[]` following a short-EPG timeout is never written to memory or disk as a servable entry (no 24 h empty); the late future attaches via `_attachLateShortEpg` → `_storeLateShortEpg`, which writes a short-EPG-sourced entry only when the channel has no programmes cached, stamps "Last updated", and bumps the guide version — a late empty/dead answer writes the withheld feed-empty `fallbackEntry` instead, so it becomes genuine only then. D1a carve-out: a feed failure after a short-EPG timeout returns empty-uncached with the late future instead of throwing (short-EPG has not answered, so "both sources failed" does not apply). The channel's `AbortController` stays registered in `_epgAbortsByChannel`/`_activeEpgAborts` while a late request is outstanding (removed on `lateShortEpg.whenComplete`), so scroll-away / background still cancels it — the background request is owned by the channel fetch, never orphaned (the 26.8 unobserved-socket concern). The record fields `shortEpgTimedOut` + `lateShortEpg` are threaded through `_fetchXtreamEpg` / `_fetchXtreamEpgWithAbort` / `_fetchFeedPrograms` / `_fetchM3uEpg` (feed/M3U paths: `false`/`null`). `_bumpEpgVersion()` helper; all four `_epgVersion++` sites converted (the bulk replace also hit the helper's own body — fixed to `_epgVersion++; epgVersionNotifier.value++;`).
- 56.2 (R3, short-EPG shape) — `lib/services/iptv/xtream_api.dart`: `getShortEpg` accepts `epg_listings` as a JSON object (values parsed) as well as a list; `XtreamEpgProgram.fromJson` falls back to parsing `start`/`stop` strings (`"2026-11-14 16:00:00"` → UTC epoch, the Xtream epoch convention — naive strings treated as UTC) when `start_timestamp`/`stop_timestamp` are missing or 0. Public `effectiveUserAgent` getter (for 56.3).
- 56.3 (R4, feed-download diagnostics) — NEW `IptvEpgFeedDownload` (`lib/services/iptv/iptv_epg_sources.dart`): bytes, SHA-256, channels/programmes parsed (before the window filter) vs retained (after), 304-vs-200, validators seen, User-Agent, fetched-at. `_loadXmltvGuide` counts bytes and hashes them incrementally as they stream past the parse tee (`crypto.sha256` chunked conversion — the temp file is never read back whole); the 304 path records `notModified: true` with 0 bytes and the carried-forward counts. `_EpgSourceAttempt` gains `userAgent` (Xtream → the API's effective User-Agent; M3U → null, no UA header sent). `_recordSourceSuccess` takes the attempt and merges the download + UA into `IptvEpgSourceStatus` (new fields `programsParsed`, `channelsParsed`, `bytesDownloaded`, `sha256`, `notModified`, `etag`, `lastModified`, `userAgent`; `toJson`/`fromJson`/`copyWith` updated — a memory-served load keeps the last download's facts, it never wipes them). `IptvReloadTvDetailsResult` gains `feedDownloads`; the Reload dialog shows one line per download (`epgReloadFeedDownload`) and the per-source status detail shows the last download (`epgSourceDownloadDetail`); the Reload `appLogger.i` line appends the download summary (that is the Logs surface — the Logs export has no EPG status section).
- 56.4 (R5, idle re-fetch) — `IptvLiveTvSupport.epgVersionNotifier` (`ValueNotifier<int>`) + `_bumpEpgVersion()`; `lib/screens/livetv/tabs/guide_tab.dart`: `_iptvEpgVersionListeners` map + `_subscribeIptvEpgVersion` (listener calls `setState` when mounted), subscribed in `initState` for initial channels AND in `_ensureIptvEpg` per built row (covers later channels), removed in `dispose`. The gap was proven by code reading: the guide tab never listened to `isPlayingNotifier` or `epgVersion` outside `_ensureIptvEpg` (row-builder only), so a version bump while the guide was open did nothing until the next scroll/window change.
- 56.5 (R6, guide-coverage sample) — NEW `IptvGuideCoverageSample` + `IptvLiveTvSupport.sampleGuideCoverage(List<LiveTvChannel> channels, {int sampleSize = 200})` (`lib/media/iptv_live_tv.dart`): the first 200 channels in guide order, counted by the source that served them (short-EPG / feed-by-id / feed-by-name / cached-empty / never-fetched), from the per-channel cache (in-memory, else the persisted file via one `_persistedEpgChannelKeys()` listing — no panel calls). `reloadTvDetails()` samples BEFORE `refreshEpgCache()` busts the cache; the result carries `guideCoverageSample` and the Reload dialog shows it (`epgReloadGuideCoverageSample`) next to the feed-only match count.
- 56.6 (R2, M3U tvg-id diagnostic ONLY — no matching change) — NEW `IptvM3uTvgIdDiagnostic` + `IptvLiveTvSupport.diagnoseM3uTvgIds()`: `XtreamApi.getM3u()` once, `compute(parseM3u, text)`, stream-id → tvg-id from `/live/` URLs only (first path segment must be `live`), no-id channels checked against the loaded-feed ids (`_loadEnabledFeeds`, no force). Never throws (error in the result); never runs automatically (tens of MB); never breaks the Xtream-only path when `get.php` fails. The EPG sources screen's EPG-details group gains a "Check M3U guide IDs" row (spinner + result dialog, `epgM3uTvgId*` strings); the outcome is logged via `appLogger.i`.
- 56.7 (tests) — NEW `test/services/iptv/iptv_epg_shortepg_1_2_15_test.dart` (R1 + R3), `iptv_epg_feed_download_1_2_15_test.dart` (R4), `iptv_epg_coverage_sample_1_2_15_test.dart` (R6), `iptv_epg_m3u_tvgid_1_2_15_test.dart` (R2), NEW `test/screens/livetv/guide_tab_epg_idle_refetch_test.dart` (R5 widget test, built on the `guide_tab_epg_cancel_test.dart` harness pattern — the 1.2.14 blank-row harness does not exist on this base); test 26.8 in `iptv_build26_lazy_feed_test.dart` rewritten to the new R1 contract (first-paint timeout does NOT abort the socket — `shortEpgAborted` stays false — then `cancelEpgFetchForChannel` aborts it). FAILING-FIRST (by construction — `flutter test` NOT run yet; the runner is held until the parent gives the go-ahead, 1.2.13 owns it, 1.2.14 is queued ahead): R1 fails on base — base aborts the request at the first-paint timeout and writes the empty entry, so the "no file after the first fetch" assertion fails and the late heal never lands; R3 fails on base — object `epg_listings` returns [] and string start/stop parse to 0/0; R4/R6/R2 do not compile on base (`IptvEpgFeedDownload`/`feedDownloads`, `sampleGuideCoverage`/`IptvGuideCoverageSample`, `diagnoseM3uTvgIds`/`IptvM3uTvgIdDiagnostic` do not exist there); R5 fails on base — no version listener, so the deferred row never re-fetches while the guide is open; rewritten 26.8 fails on base — base aborts at the first-paint timeout, so `shortEpgAborted` is true within ~1 s. `flutter analyze`: 53 = base 53, zero issues in any touched file (two real errors caught and fixed during development: `AccumulatorSink` is gone from this Dart SDK's dart:convert — replaced with a local `_DigestCollector` sink for the chunked SHA-256 — and `getM3u`'s real signature carries `{type, output}` named params).
- Version: 1.2.15-2.25.0+209. Changelog `about.changelog2301` (5 entries, one per fix). UNPROVEN (phone-only): R1's gain — whether slow short-EPG answers actually heal rows in the background on the real panel (phone check H3: tap-to-retry an empty row with nothing playing); the Reload sample + download lines on the real feed; the M3U diagnostic numbers; About 1.2.15-2.25.0; the updater offering it and not re-offering after install. NO RSS BOUND claimed. The honest feed ceiling (~3,396) is unchanged by this build — the guide is NOT "fixed", only the matcher/reporting and the slow-answer behaviour.

## 57. TV Logs D-pad focus + hub-load OOM + any-crash saver (v1.2.16-2.25.0, versionCode 210)
